ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FOR LAND LOCATED SOUTHWEST OF THE INTERSECTION OF 
WILD BLUFF AND OGDEN ROAD FROM MR MEDIUM-DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL TO NR-2 NEIGHBORHOOD RESIDENTIAL – MEDIUM 
DENSITY." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on March 3, 2025, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned zoning map amendment application is hereby approved and the zoning 
classification of the real property described as Lot 1, Block 2, The Bluffs, 12th Filing, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, is hereby changed to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density.  
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

Medium Density

AGENT:

PUDC-25-34 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 1, Block 2, The Bluffs 12th Filing to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential 
– Medium Density as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density for roughly 10.92 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. 
There is an associated plat with this zone change which would subdivide the property for Cottage Lot style 
development. The plat application is PUDC-25-35. Specific Lot layout may change with review of the plat, but 
the substance of the request will stay the same.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
infrastructure that is currently being built.

PUDC-25-34 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential and the surrounding area 
is Urban Residential, Mixed-use Residential, and Public. According to the comprehensive plan 
the optimal City zones for the subject property’s future land use designation is any one of the 
residential zones, including NR-2. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, NR-2 zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Surrounding properties in the City are zoned MR, CB, NR-3, and P. MR and NR-2 zones are 
medium-density residential zones. The difference is that NR-2 zoning anticipates alley-loaded 
development, allows smaller lots, more urban styles of development, and has a higher design 
standard. NR-2 zoning is compatible with surrounding zoning.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The MR, as put forward in the UDC, is intended for larger-block, front-loaded, suburban style 
development patterns (like Saddle Ridge or The Pointe). NR-2 zoning is intended for smaller-
block, rear-loaded, compact development patterns (like what is found in the Avenues). The 
proposed development is for a cottage lot style of development using single-family detached 
homes on smaller lots which better fits in the NR-2 zone. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is occurring in an area that has recently been platted with a major street 
network. No changes to the major street network are proposed. There will be a new street and 
alleys added with the plat for the neighborhood.  
 
Infrastructure (streets and utilities) are being built in the area. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The NR-2 zone is intended for a medium density and mix of dwelling types that promote a 
consistent neighborhood character, with a compact development pattern and highly connected 

PUDC-25-34 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
street network. The proposed project is a cottage lot development which proposes a more 
compact type of development. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The NR-2 zone allows a variety of residential uses (as does the current MR zone). The applicant 
intends to build a cottage lot style development which is permitted in the NR-2 zone. The NR-2 
also has design standards required per UDC 6.6.4 which will need to be met for the cottage lot 
development or any other type of development that is proposed for this area. Should this 
particular project not move forward with cottage lots, the NR-2 zoning provides other options 
for residential development. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Proposal meets comprehensive plan principles. No other comments were received from 
reviewers.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED: 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the 
review criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways [only those applicable to the application 
are listed]: 
• 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on February 15, 2025 
• 
Mailed Notice to neighbors within 300’, sent on February 14, 2025 (Mailed by applicant or their 
agent) 

PUDC-25-34 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
• 
Posted Notice on the property is not required until February 24, 2025. 
• 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on February 14, 2025. 
 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (February 24, 2025), Staff has received no inquires or comments 
from the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 21 views and no 
comments.  
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no major review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The NR-2 zone 
is appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, proposed development. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MR to NR-2.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN 
ORDINANCE 
AMENDING 
SECTION 
10.24.190, 
CRITICAL 
TRAFFIC CONTROL AREAS; CREATION AND ADMINISTRATION; 
ENHANCED PENALTIES, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, 
MODIFYING 
THE 
PROVISIONS 
GOVERNING THE CREATION AND ENFORCEMENT OF SUCH 
AREAS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.24.190, Critical Traffic Control Areas; Creation and 
Administration; Enhanced Penalties, of Chapter 10.24, Rules of the Road Generally, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is amended to read: 
 
10.24.190 - Critical traffic control areas; creation and administration; enhanced penalties. 
 
A. There is created the critical traffic control program, which shall be empowered to designate 
one (1) or more critical traffic control areas in the city of Cheyenne as provided in this 
section. The program shall be administered by the program committee in accordance with 
this section, which committee shall be composed of the mayor or his or her designee, the 
city engineer or his or her designee, and the chief of police or his or her designee. The 
objective of the program shall be to identify areas in the city with unsafe or unlawful traffic 
speeds or other behaviors and to implement appropriate temporary remedial measures to 
address those behaviors. 
 
B. No A critical traffic control area shall may only be designated under the program except as 
provided in this in accordance with subsection (C) of this section and only after the receipt 
of a critical traffic control program complaint. The complaint shall be signed by not less 
than five (5) residents of the area that is the subject of the complaint, which residents shall 
represent different businesses or households affected by the alleged unsafe or unlawful 
traffic speeds or behaviors. Complaint forms shall be made available to the public by the 
city engineer. Any complaint submitted under this subsection shall specifically identify the 
unsafe or unlawful traffic speeds or behaviors that are of concern to the residents. 
 
C. The program committee shall review and evaluate the merits of any traffic control program 
complaint submitted in accordance with subsection (B) of this section. Should the 
committee determine the complaint has merit, the city engineer shall order a traffic study 
for the affected area, the results of which shall be submitted to the committee not less than 
fourteen (14) days after the date that the study was initiated. If the city engineer ordered a 
study to evaluate vehicle speeds, the study period shall include not less than two (2) 
weekdays and not less than one (1) weekend Saturday as data gathering points. In addition, 
a crash history for not less than the previous five (5) years shall be obtained and a crash 
data analysis shall be developed and summarized. If the results of the study substantiate 
the complaint, the program committee may designate the affected area as a critical traffic 
10:26 am, Mar 19, 2025

 
Page 2 of 4 
 
control area. Nothing in this subsection shall be construed to prohibit the city engineer from 
commissioning a third-party professional engineer to complete the traffic study required 
under this subsection. 
 
D. Upon the designation of any critical traffic control area as provided by subsection (C) (B) 
of this section and subject to the requirements of subsection (F), the program committee 
may implement appropriate temporary remedial measures to address the unlawful traffic 
speeds or other behaviors including: 
 
1. Establishing enhanced fines for speeding violations within the designated area, which 
fines shall be two (2) times the amount of the fine that would be imposed for the same 
violation in the same or similar area but for the traffic control area designation. If 
enhanced fines are implemented, proper signage shall also be installed to provide 
reasonable notice to drivers, which signage shall identify the beginning and end points 
of the critical traffic control area. An enhanced fine imposed under this paragraph is 
mandatory and may only be reduced by a municipal court judge as follows: 
 
a. after the court specifically finds Upon a specific finding that the defendant has no 
ability to pay the full fine amount and that no reasonable probability exists that the 
defendant will have an ability to pay the full fine amount; 
 
b. Upon completion of a defensive driving course but only if the defendant has 
previously maintained a motor vehicle record free of moving violations and the 
prosecutor first consents; or 
 
c. The defendant is entitled to a fine reduction under W.S. 31-5-1402(e). 
 
2. Installation of additional traffic control devices, including driver feedback radar signs, 
traffic control signals or traffic calming devices; 
 
3. Increased law enforcement presence for the purpose of strictly enforcing traffic laws, 
which increased presence may be incentivized through the authorization of overtime to 
be offered to police officers; and 
 
4. Any other appropriate temporary measures the committee determines may address the 
unsafe or unlawful traffic behaviors. 
 
E. Any critical traffic control area designated under this section shall be reevaluated by the 
program committee not more than fourteen (14) thirty (30) days after the implementation 
date of any temporary remedial measures to determine if the measures are still appropriate 
under the circumstances. Should the committee determine the unsafe or unlawful traffic 
behaviors have been corrected, the critical traffic control area designation shall terminate 
and the measures implemented in accordance with this section shall be removed. Should 
remain appropriate or should be modified or terminated. Record of the program 
committee's revaluation shall be filed in accordance with subsection (F) of this section. The 
program committee’s failure to meet within the required thirty (30) days shall not affect 

 
Page 3 of 4 
 
the validity of the critical traffic control area or the enforcement authority for any enhanced 
fine implemented under paragraph (D)(1) of this section. If the committee determine that 
the temporary remedial measures should be modified or continued, modifies or continues 
the remedial measures, the area shall again be reevaluated by the program committee not 
more than fourteen (14) thirty (30) days after the implementation date that the measures 
were most recently modified or continued. Any Except for enhanced fines under paragraph 
(D)(1) of this section, any remedial measure otherwise implemented under this section may 
be instituted on a made permanent basis when necessary to maintain the public safety of 
the previously designated critical traffic control area and if made permanent, shall not be 
subject to the reevaluation requirements imposed by this subsection. 
 
F. Upon the designation of a critical traffic control area and the implementation of any 
temporary remedial measures under subsections (C) and (D) of this section, or the 
modification or termination of an existing area under subsection (E), the program 
committee shall file a record of the action with the city clerk using a standardized form. 
The form shall specify the remedial measures to be implemented, continued or modified 
and further state the effective date for the implementation, continuance or modification of 
applicable remedial measures. If a designation is terminated, the form shall specify the 
effective date of the termination. Upon receipt of a record of action form under this 
subsection, the city clerk shall provide a copy to the chief of police or his or her designee 
for the purposes of enforcement. 
 
F. G. 
Nothing in this section shall be construed to limit or otherwise affect the authority 
granted under title 10, chapter 10.68 of the city code or the authority of the city traffic 
engineer under section 10.12.130 of the city code.  
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 3.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-03-04_TotalExtraction_51105515.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: PLTA-24-4: An ordinance amending Chapter 10.82, Motor-assisted Scooters, Electric 
Bicycles, and Bicycles Equipped with GPS of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, authorizing dockless vehicle companies to conduct 
business in the city using the city's formal procurement process, making a conforming 
amendment and repealing obsolete provisions. 
Date: 
March 24, 2025 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is an amendment to municipal code and is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral:  
March 24, 2025 
• 
Finance Committee:  
 
April 8, 2025 
• 
Second reading: 
 
April 14, 2025 
• 
Finance Committee:  
 
April 23, 2025 
• 
Third and final reading:   
April 21, 2025 
 
This ordinance establishes a formal process for selecting a single dockless vehicle (electric scooters) 
company to operate in Cheyenne through a Request for Proposal (RFP) system, aiming to improve 
communication with the provider and ensure market viability. Key RFP criteria include fleet size, 
operations and maintenance plans, safety measures, data sharing, community engagement, compliance, 
and potential revenue to the City. 
 
The new system addresses challenges from the current model, where unlimited providers make City 
coordination difficult and market fragmentation prevents operators from offsetting costs. Additionally, 
the current model only requires a one-time $200 annual license fee per provider and a $5 annual fee per 
dockless vehicle. 
 
Currently, dockless vehicle providers operate on a month-to-month license issued by the City Clerk’s 
Office, with only one provider actively serving the City. They and other providers are awaiting the 
implementation of the RFP process and intend to submit proposals when it’s posted. The RFP is set to be 
posted upon ordinance approval, with a new provider expected to be in place by June of this year. 
 
Attachment: 
1. Ordinance 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTER 10.82, MOTOR-ASSISTED 
SCOOTERS, ELECTRIC BICYCLES, AND BICYCLES EQUIPPED WITH 
GPS OF TITLE 10, VEHICLES AND TRAFFIC, OF THE MUNICIPAL 
CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, AUTHORIZING 
DOCKLESS VEHICLE COMPANIES TO CONDUCT BUSINESS IN THE 
CITY USING THE CITY'S FORMAL PROCUREMENT PROCESS, 
MAKING 
A 
CONFORMING 
AMENDMENT 
AND 
REPEALING 
OBSOLETE PROVISIONS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Sections 10.82.090 through 10.82.130, of Chapter 10.82, Motor-Assisted 
Scooters, Electric Bicycles, and Bicycles Equipped with GPS, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby created to read: 
 
10.82.090 - Findings and Purpose 
 
A. Certain companies around the nation provide dockless vehicles that can be ridden by 
patrons for payment of a fee. 
 
B. The regulation of dockless vehicles is important to promote safe use, limit conflict with 
other users and to efficiently manage the use of the city's right-of-way and multi-use paths. 
 
C. The City of Cheyenne recognizes the growing interest and demand for alternative 
transportation options, including dockless vehicles such as electric bicycles and scooters. 
 
D. The city aims to provide safe, reliable and efficient transportation alternatives for its 
residents and visitors and seeks to implement a structured and transparent process to select 
a qualified company to provide dockless vehicle services through a Request for Proposal 
(RFP) process. 
 
10.82.100 - Definitions. 
 
A. As used in this chapter: 
 
1. "City" means the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming; 
 
2. "Dockless vehicle" means any bicycle, scooter or other personal transportation device 
that does not require a fixed docking station for parking; 
 
3. "Operator" means a person selected through the RFP process to provide dockless 
vehicle services in the city of Cheyenne, which person has a current operating 
agreement with the city;" 
9:59 am, Mar 19, 2025

 
Page 2 of 12 
 
4. "Person" includes an individual, partnership, corporation, joint stock company or any 
other association or entity, public or private; and 
 
5. "Request for Proposal" or "RFP" means a competitive process that invites persons 
engaged in the dockless vehicles business to participate in the city's formal procurement 
process and submit proposals for providing dockless vehicle services. 
 
10.82.110 - Dockless vehicles; Request for Proposal process. 
 
A. When appropriate, the city shall issue an RFP to invite proposals from any person interested 
in providing dockless vehicle services. The RFP shall outline the requirements, evaluation 
criteria, scope of services and other conditions for providing the service. Persons 
responding to the RFP shall submit a comprehensive proposal detailing their ability to meet 
the requirements set forth in the RFP, which requirements shall include those specified in 
this subsection. Any person who fails to submit a proposal or who does not follow the 
requirements of the Request for Proposal shall not be considered. The requirements of the 
RFP shall include: 
 
1. Fleet size and vehicle specifications; 
 
2. Operations and maintenance plans, including a description of how the proposed 
operator will respond: 
 
a.  To complaints of improperly parked or abandoned dockless vehicles; and 
 
b. Reported safety or operational concerns of any deployed dockless vehicle; 
 
3. Safety measures and rider education; 
 
4. Data sharing and reporting capabilities; 
 
5. Community engagement strategies;  
 
6. Compliance with local, state, and federal regulations;  
 
7. Programs for reduced rates; and 
 
8. Any other requirement deemed appropriate by the Mayor or his or her designee. 
 
B. RFP proposals shall be evaluated based on criteria established in the RFP, which may 
include: 
 
1. Experience and qualifications of the prospective operator; 
 
2. Quality and sustainability of the dockless vehicles; 
 

 
Page 3 of 12 
 
3. Robustness of the operations and maintenance plan; 
 
4. Commitment to safety and rider education; 
 
5. Ability to provide reliable data and reporting;  
 
6. Community engagement and responsiveness; 
 
7. Programs for reduced rates;  
 
8. Potential revenue to the city to be provided by prospective operator; and 
 
9. Any other criteria deemed appropriate by the Mayor or his or her designee. 
 
C. The city shall review and evaluate all proposals received in response to the RFP in 
accordance with the procedures established in the RFP and the city's formal procurement 
process. The city may select one (1) or more operators based on the proposals received and 
on the evaluation criteria of the RFP. The city shall enter into an operating agreement with 
each selected operator, which agreement shall specify the terms and conditions of 
providing dockless vehicle services. Nothing in this subsection shall be construed to require 
the city to select any of the RFP submissions. 
 
10.82.120 - Operator responsibilities; regulations and compliance 
 
A. Any operators selected through the RFP process shall be responsible for: 
 
1. Complying with all terms and conditions set forth in the operating agreement with the 
city; 
 
2. Ensuring the safe and efficient operation of dockless vehicles; 
 
3. Regularly maintaining and servicing the dockless vehicles; 
 
4. Providing customer service and addressing any issues or complaints promptly; and 
 
5. Sharing data with the city as required for monitoring and evaluation purposes. 
 
B. The city reserves the right to establish additional regulations governing the operation of 
dockless vehicles and to enforce compliance with all applicable laws and regulations. 
Failure to comply with applicable laws and regulations may result in penalties, including 
but not limited to fines, suspension or revocation of the operator's authority to provide 
dockless vehicle services. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 10.80.030, Traffic Ordinances Apply to Persons Riding Bicycles, 
of Chapter 10.80, Bicycles and Motor-Assisted Scooters, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, is 
hereby amened to read: 

 
Page 4 of 12 
 
 
10.80.030 - Traffic ordinances apply to persons riding bicycles, scooters and dockless 
vehicles. 
 
Every person riding a bicycle, or motor-assisted scooter or dockless vehicle as defined by 
Section 10.82.100 of the city code, upon a roadway shall be granted all of the rights and shall be 
subject to all of the duties applicable to the driver of a vehicle by this title, except as to special 
regulations in this chapter and except as to those provisions of this title which by their nature can 
have no application. 
 
Section 3.  That Sections 10.82.010 through 10.82.080, of Chapter 10.82, Motor-Assisted 
Scooters, Electric Bicycles, and Bicycles Equipped with GPS, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby repealed. 
 
10.82.010 - Effect of regulations. 
 
A. It is a misdemeanor for any person to do any act forbidden or fail to perform any act 
required in this chapter. 
 
B. These regulations applicable to motor-assisted scooters, electric bicycles, and bicycles 
equipped with GPS shall apply whenever a motor-assisted scooter, electric bicycle, or 
bicycle equipped with GPS is operated within the jurisdiction of the city of Cheyenne 
subject to those exceptions stated herein.  
 
10.82.020 - Definitions and exclusions. 
 
As used in this chapter, the following words and phrases and any variations thereof, shall have 
the following meaning: 
 
"Bicycle equipped with GPS" means a device that a person may ride and that is propelled by 
human power and has two tandem wheels, at least one of which is more than fourteen (14) inches 
in diameter, which has a global positioning system (GPS) to determine the geographic location of 
the bicycle. 
 
"Director" means the director of the compliance department and/or the city clerk, as applicable, 
designated to enforce and administer this chapter and includes representatives, agents, or 
department employees designated by the director. 
 
"Dockless vehicle" means a motor-assisted scooter, an electric bicycle, or a bicycle equipped 
with GPS, owned by a non-governmental entity, that is available for use for a fee. 
 
"Electric bicycle" means a bicycle as defined in Wyo. Stat. Section 31-1-101(a)(xxxiv). 
 
"Fleet manager" means the person responsible for the daily operations of a dockless vehicle 
service, who must be based in the city. 
 

 
Page 5 of 12 
 
"Geofencing" means the use of a virtual perimeter in which the use of a dockless vehicle is 
either restricted or the speed of the dockless vehicle is reduced when in use. 
 
"Motor-assisted scooter" means a self-propelled device, not including a pocket bike or mini-
motorbike, with:  
 
1.  At least two wheels in contact with the ground during operation; 
 
2.  A braking system capable of stopping the device under typical operating conditions; 
 
3.  A gas or electric motor not exceeding forty (40) cubic centimeters; 
 
4.  A deck designed to allow a person to stand or sit while operating the device; and 
 
5.  The ability to be propelled by human power alone. 
 
"License" means the business license issued by the city to a person which authorizes the 
operation of a service providing dockless vehicles for compensation. 
 
"License holder" means the person who owns the operation if a sole proprietorship, or the 
person who has been designated as managing the operation if any other entity, of a service 
providing dockless vehicles for compensation.  
 
"Person" means an individual, partnership, corporation, company, association, or other legal 
entity.  
 
This chapter does not apply to vehicles as defined by Wyoming Statutes, to include:  
 
1. Wyo. Stat. Section 31-1-101(a)(xv) [Motor Vehicle]; and 
 
2. Wyo. Stat. Section 31-1-101(a)(xxvi) [Vehicle]. 
 
10.82.030 - Dockless vehicle license . 
 
A. A person must register and obtain a license from the city, and pay any applicable fees, prior 
to providing a dockless vehicle for any compensation including, but not limited to, any 
money, thing of value, payment, consideration, donation, gratuity, or profit.  
 
B. Unless otherwise specified under subsection (F) of this section, to obtain a license, a person 
must submit an application to the city clerk's office on a form provided by the city clerk's 
office for that purpose. The application must contain the following, including any 
additional information and requirements established by the director:  
 
1. The business name, street address, mailing address, e-mail address, and telephone 
number of the applicant; 
 

 
Page 6 of 12 
 
2. A non-refundable annual license fee of two hundred dollars ($200.00); 
 
3. A non-refundable annual license fee of five dollars ($5.00) for each dockless vehicle 
which is to be operated within the city limits of the city of Cheyenne; 
 
4. The name, telephone number, and e-mail address of the fleet manager. Any change in 
this information must be reported to the director within twenty-four (24) hours of the 
change; 
 
5. A phone number for the public to report improperly parked dockless vehicles and other 
violations, which must be posted on each dockless vehicle; 
 
6. The address of the fixed facilities to be used in the operation, if any, and the address of 
the applicant's headquarters, if different from the address of the fixed facilities; 
 
7. Documentary evidence from an insurance company indicating that such insurance 
company has bound itself to provide the applicant with the liability insurance required 
by this chapter; 
 
8. Documentary evidence of payment of ad valorem and sales taxes on property within 
the city, if any, to be used in connection with the operation of the proposed dockless 
vehicle program; 
 
9. The number and the types of dockless vehicles to be operated; and 
 
10. An agreement to indemnify the city. 
 
C. Except as provided in subsection (B)(4) of this section, an applicant or registrant shall 
notify the director, in the manner prescribed by the director, within ten (10) days of any 
change in the information contained in the application for a license. If the information 
reported to the director includes an increase in the number of dockless vehicles, any 
additional fees due must be submitted to the director simultaneously with the change in 
information. 
 
D. Unless otherwise specified under subsection (F) of this section, a license expires one year 
from the date it is issued. An applicant may renew a license following the process in this 
section. 
 
E. A license is non-transferrable. This regulation should not be construed to impede the 
continuing use of trade names. 
 
F. Notwithstanding any licensing provision to the contrary imposed under this section or 
Section 10.82.040 of this chapter, any person seeking to provide a dockless vehicle for any 
compensation on or after June 1, 2024, may only do so upon receipt of a license issued by 
the city clerk's office, which license shall only be effective for a term of one (1) month 
from the time of issuance and shall be subject to the following conditions and requirements: 

 
Page 7 of 12 
 
 
1. The monthly license authorized under this subsection may be renewed on a monthly 
basis subject to any additional information and requirements established by the 
director; 
 
2. A non-refundable monthly license fee of seventeen dollars ($17.00) shall be submitted 
by the applicant prior to the issuance or renewal of any license under this subsection; 
 
3. A non-refundable monthly fee of fifty cents ($0.50) for each dockless vehicle which is 
to be operated within the city limits of the city of Cheyenne shall be submitted by the 
applicant prior to the issuance of any license or renewal under this subsection; and 
 
4. The director shall not be required to approve any license application or renewal 
application submitted under this subsection, regardless of whether the applicant 
otherwise qualifies for a license or to have a license renewed, if the approval would be 
contrary to the public interest. Any application that is denied under this paragraph shall 
not be entitled to administrative review by the city. 
 
10.82.040 - Issuance, renewal, suspension, and revocation of license. 
 
A. The director shall refuse to issue or renew a license if the applicant:  
 
1. Does not meet the requirements in the license application, including failing to meet any 
requirements established by the director; 
 
2. Intentionally or knowingly makes a false statement as to a material matter in an 
application for a license; 
 
3. Has been convicted twice within a twelve-month period for a violation of this chapter 
or has had a license revoked within two years of the date of application; or 
 
4. Is applying for or seeks to renew an annual license under Section 10.82.030 on or after 
June 1, 2024. 
 
B. If the director denies a license, the director shall notify the applicant or operator in writing 
that the application is denied and include in the notice the specific reason or reasons for 
denial and a statement informing the applicant or operator of the right to appeal the 
decision, pursuant to the city's Administrative Hearing Procedure, Chapter 2.92 of the city 
code. The right to appeal specified in this subsection, shall not apply to the denial of any 
license under Section 10.82.030(F)(4) of this chapter. 
 
C. The director shall revoke a license if the director determines that the license holder has: 
 
1. Made a false statement as to a material matter in the application; 
 
2. Failed to maintain the insurance required by this chapter; 

 
Page 8 of 12 
 
 
3. Operated dockless vehicles in excess of the number authorized by the license; or 
 
4. Failed to pay a fee required by this chapter. 
 
D. After revocation of a license, an operator is not eligible for another license for a period of 
up to two years, depending on the severity of the violation resulting in the revocation, as 
determined by the director. 
 
E. Except as otherwise provided by Section 10.82.030(F) of this chapter, any person whose 
application for a license, or renewal of a license, is denied by the director, or an operator 
whose license has been revoked or suspended by the director, may file an appeal with the 
city clerk in accordance with the city's administrative hearing procedure, Chapter 2.92 of 
the city code. 
 
10.82.050 - Dockless vehicle operations. 
 
A. Each dockless vehicle permitted under this chapter must clearly display the emblem of the 
license holder, a unique identification number, and a twenty-four-hour phone number for 
customers and citizens to report safety concerns, make complaints, ask questions, or 
request a dockless vehicle be relocated. 
 
B. The fleet manager, or a designated representative, shall be available by the telephone 
number provided on the application, seven days a week during all hours of operation. 
 
C. License holders shall not attach any personal property (other than dockless vehicles), 
fixtures, or structures to the public right-of-way without the separate written permission of 
the director. Any permission to place items in the public right-of-way must be incorporated 
into the license. 
 
D. License holders shall provide the director electronic access to the current list of dockless 
vehicles available for rent in the city, which includes the unique identification number for 
each dockless vehicle. 
 
E. License holders shall educate customers regarding the law applicable to riding, operating, 
and parking a dockless vehicle. A license holder's application must provide information 
notifying the user that: 
 
1. The use of helmets is encouraged for the safety of the drivers; 
 
2. The legal parking of dockless vehicles; 
 
3. The legal operation of dockless vehicles, including the duty to yield to pedestrians; and 
 
4. For motor-assisted scooters and electric bicycles, the areas where riding and parking 
are prohibited. 

 
Page 9 of 12 
 
 
F. Notices of violations or broken or inoperable dockless vehicles: 
 
1. License holders shall have two hours after receiving notice of a violation, from any 
source, to correct violations for a dockless vehicle being parked in an area prohibited 
under this chapter; for other violations, the license holder has two hours after receiving 
notice to correct the violation. 
 
2. License holders are required to lock dockless vehicles reported as broken, from any 
source, and must remove the dockless vehicle within two hours. 
 
3. After the time for correcting a violation has expired, the city may remove and impound 
a dockless vehicle that is parked in violation of this chapter or broken. The license 
holder must pay the city a fee of fifty dollars ($50.00) to obtain the return of each 
dockless vehicle impounded. 
 
G. License holders shall provide the collection and analysis of aggregate data concerning its 
operations to the city: 
 
1. License holders shall provide a monthly report to the director of the maximum number 
of permitted dockless vehicles in use by riders at any time in the previous week; and 
 
2. License holders shall provide a monthly report to the director that includes: 
 
a. The total number of rides the previous month; 
 
b. The total number of dockless vehicles in service for the previous month; 
 
c. The average number of rides per dockless vehicle per day; 
 
d. Anonymized aggregated data taken by the license holder's dockless vehicles in the 
form of heat maps showing routes, trends, origins, and destinations; and 
 
e. Anonymized trip data taken by the license holder's dockless vehicles that includes 
the origin and destination, trip duration, distance, and date and time of trip. 
 
H. License holders shall provide such other reports as the director may request. 
 
I. License holders shall adhere to geofencing requirements, as determined and at the 
discretion of the director. 
 
10.82.060 - Insurance. 
 
A. A license holder shall procure and keep in full force and effect no less than the insurance 
coverage required by this section through a policy or policies written by insurance company 
that: 

 
Page 10 of 12 
 
 
1. Is authorized to do business in the State of Wyoming; 
 
2. Is acceptable to the city; and 
 
3. Does not violate the ownership or operational control prohibition described in this 
section. 
 
B. The insured provisions of the policy must name the city and its officers and employees as 
additional insureds, and the coverage provisions must provide coverage for any loss or 
damage that may arise to any person or property by reason of the operation of a dockless 
vehicle. 
 
C. A license holder shall maintain insurance and provide indemnification in accordance with 
the indemnification and insurance agreement required by the director and on file with the 
compliance department and the city clerk. Additionally: 
 
1. If a license holder will utilize motor vehicles in its operations, the business automotive 
liability insurance must cover owned, hired, and non-owned vehicles, with a combined 
single limit for bodily injury (including death) and property damage of five hundred 
thousand dollars ($500,000.00) per occurrence, and no annual aggregate. 
 
2. Workers' compensation will be provided in accordance with Wyoming law. 
 
D. Insurance required under this chapter must: 
 
1. Include a cancellation provision in which the insurance company is required to notify 
the director in writing not fewer than thirty (30) days before cancelling the insurance 
policy (for a reason other than non-payment) or before making a reduction in coverage; 
 
2. Include a cancellation provision in which the insurance company is required to notify 
the director in writing not fewer than ten (10) days before cancelling for non-payment; 
 
3. Cover all dockless vehicles during the times that the dockless vehicles are deployed or 
operating in furtherance of the license holder's business; 
 
4. Include a provision requiring the insurance company to pay every covered claim on a 
first-dollar basis; 
 
5. Require notice to the director if the policy is cancelled or if there is a reduction in 
coverage; and 
 
6. Comply with all applicable federal, state, and local laws. 
 
E. No person who has a twenty (20) percent or greater ownership interest in the license holder 
may have an interest in the insurance company. 

 
Page 11 of 12 
 
 
F. A license holder may not be self-insured. 
 
G. Any insurance policy required by this chapter must be on file with and approved by the 
city prior to the issuance of a license, must remain in effect during the term of the license. 
 
10.82.070 - Enforcement. 
 
A. The responsibility of enforcement of this chapter shall be shared by the police department, 
city clerk, compliance department, and the planning and development department, as 
applicable. 
 
B. Upon observing a violation of this chapter, the director shall take necessary action to ensure 
effective regulation of dockless vehicles. 
 
C. The director may, with or without notice, inspect any dockless vehicle operating under this 
chapter to determine whether the dockless vehicle complies with this chapter and other 
applicable laws. 
 
10.82.080 - Criminal offenses. 
 
A. A person commits an offense if, within the city, the person operates or causes or permits 
the operation of a dockless vehicle service without a valid license issued under this chapter. 
 
B. A person commits an offense if the person violates or attempts to violate a provision of this 
chapter. 
 
C. A culpable mental state is not required for the commission of an offense under this chapter. 
A separate offense is committed each day in which an offense occurs. 
 
D. Prosecution for an offense under this chapter does not prevent the use of other enforcement 
remedies or procedures applicable to the person charged with the conduct or involved in 
the offense. 
 
E. Any person who violates any of the provisions of this chapter shall be guilty of a 
misdemeanor and upon adjudication or conviction thereof shall be fined in an amount not 
to exceed seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00) and costs or imprisonment in jail for a 
period not to exceed six months, or both, pursuant to Section 2.40.030 of the city code. 
 
Section 4.  Upon the effective date of this ordinance as specified by Section 6, the city clerk 
shall not renew the dockless vehicle license held by any person. Any person holding a dockless 
vehicle license on the effective date of this ordinance shall have not more than forty (40) days after 
the expiration date of the person's dockless vehicle license to remove any and all property 
associated with the person's operation of a dockless vehicle in the city of Cheyenne. Any property 
remaining after the forty (40) day period provided in this section shall be deemed abandoned and 
the city may dispose of the property in accordance with state and local laws. 

 
Page 12 of 12 
 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-03-04_TotalExtraction_81052113.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
March 4, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Investment Policy Review and Update 
 
 
On January 28, 2025, the City's Investment Committee voted to recommend approval of revisions 
to the City's Investment Policy. The voting members of the committee include Mayor Patrick 
Collins, City Council President Mark Rinne, City Councilman Tom Segrave, and the City 
Treasurer. 
 
Mr. Jeffrey Prince, a local investment expert with Wells Fargo Advisors, serves as a non-voting 
member of this Committee and reviewed and provided feedback on the proposed policy changes. 
The City's Investment Advisor, Federated Hermes, also reviewed the policy to ensure compliance 
with Wyoming State Statutes. 
 
The proposed revisions enhance clarity and align with best practices (e.g., collateralization 
standards, liquidity management).  Significant changes include the following: 
 
Page 5 Revisions 
 
Added Local Government Investment Pools 
• 
Wyoming CLASS and Wyoming Government Investment Fund are now explicitly listed 
as authorized local government investment pools in Wyoming, alongside WyoSTAR I and 
II. 
Collateralization Requirements for Certificate of Deposits (CDs) 
• 
Collateral must equal 105% of the market value of the principal and accrued interest for 
CDs, excluding FDIC insured amounts. 
• 
Monthly Verification: The City Treasurer must now confirm that pledged collateral 
maintains sufficient value to meet this requirement. 
 
Page 6 Revisions 
 
Diversification Limit 
• 
5% Obligor Limit: No single obligor (excluding federal government, authorized pools, or 
CDs) may exceed 5% of the City’s total investment portfolio. 

2 
 
Liquidity Flexibility 
• 
Removed Six-Month Budgeted Expenditure Rule: The portfolio must remain sufficiently 
liquid to meet reasonably anticipated operating needs, rather than adhering to a rigid six-
month budgeted expenditure threshold.  
 
Page 7 Revisions 
 
Benchmark Clarification 
• 
Federated Hermes Benchmark: Updated to reflect Federated Hermes’ standard benchmark 
for accurate performance measurement. 
 
Pool Terminology 
• 
Replaced “WyoSTAR” with “one of the local government investment pools” to reflect 
broader participation options. 
 
Reporting Requirements 
• 
Bi-Annual Reports: The City Treasurer must now submit investment reports to 
the Investment Committee and Governing Body every six months, ensuring transparency 
and accountability. 
 
Please feel free to contact me at 637-6336 or email at rlockman@cheyennecity.org if you have 
questions.  

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION ADOPTING THE REVISED CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, INVESTMENT POLICY STATEMENT.” 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 9-4-831(h) requires the City of Cheyenne to adopt and 
maintain on file an Investment Policy Statement which applies to the investment of the 
financial assets of all funds of the City, except funds held in checking accounts and funds under 
the control of the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities; 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne last amended its Investment 
Policy Statement through Resolution No. 6210 on February 28, 2022;  
WHEREAS, the City's Investment Committee, comprising of the Mayor and/or their 
designee, two (2) City Council members appointed by the Council President, and the City 
Treasurer as voting members, recommends that the Governing Body approve the revised City of 
Cheyenne Investment Policy Statement attached to this Resolution; and 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body wishes to adopt the revised Investment Policy Statement 
as recommended by the Investment Committee. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: that the revised City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Investment Policy 
Statement, attached to this Resolution, is hereby adopted and shall take effect immediately upon 
passage of this Resolution.

4:59 pm, Feb 24, 2025

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
 
The purpose of this Investment Policy Statement is to establish investment objectives, provide 
investment guidelines, and set forth responsibilities and procedures necessary for the prudent 
management and investment of the funds of the City of Cheyenne (“City”).   
 
I. 
Governing Authority 
 
This Investment Policy is adopted pursuant to W.S. § 9-4-831(h). The investment program 
governed by this Policy shall be operated in conformance with all federal, state, and local legal 
and policy requirements, including the requirements set forth in W.S. § 9-4-816 through 
W.S. § 9-4-831. 
 
II. 
Scope 
 
This Policy applies to the activities of the City regarding investing the financial assets of all funds, 
except funds held in checking accounts and funds under the control of the City of Cheyenne Board 
of Public Utilities.   
 
Except for funds in certain restricted and special funds or accounts, the City may commingle its 
funds to maximize investment earnings and to increase efficiencies regarding investment pricing, 
safekeeping and administration. Investment income will be allocated to the various funds based on 
their respective participation and in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles.   
 
This Policy also applies to all commingled funds. 
 
III. 
General Objectives 
 
Investment activities shall occur in a manner that strives for the highest possible risk-adjusted total 
return consistent with the following primary objectives, in priority order:  
 
1. 
Safety:  The safety and preservation of principal is the primary and foremost objective of 
the investment program. Investments shall be made that seek to ensure the preservation of 
capital in the overall portfolio. The goal will be to mitigate credit risk, interest rate risk, 
and custodial risk.   
2. 
Liquidity:  The investment portfolio shall remain sufficiently liquid to meet all operating 
requirements that may be reasonably anticipated. This shall be accomplished by 
maintaining appropriate balances in investment vehicles that fall within the most liquid 
asset classes; and structuring the portfolio so that securities mature concurrent with cash 
needs to meet anticipated demands.  
3. 
Yield:  The investment portfolio shall be managed to maximize the return on investments 
consistent with the safety and liquidity objectives described above, with the goal of 
exceeding the stated benchmark returns over a full market cycle.  
4. 
Local Community Investment:  The investment portfolio shall be managed with a 
commitment towards local economic development. Investments in local institutions, 
domestic instruments, or through local brokers, where possible, are preferred.   
 
 
4:59 pm, Feb 24, 2025

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
2 
 
IV. 
Delegation of Authority and Responsibilities 
 
Governing Body:  The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne retains ultimate fiduciary 
responsibility for the City’s financial assets, investment program and this Policy. Upon its request, 
the Governing Body shall be given reports about the City’s investments. A copy of this Policy 
shall be reviewed and approved by the Governing Body on, at least, a biennial basis. 
 
Investment Officer:  Authority to manage the City’s investment program is granted to the City 
Treasurer, also referred to herein as the City’s Investment Officer, who shall act in accordance 
with this Policy, established written procedures, and all internal controls established for the 
operation of the investment program.   
 
Investment Committee:  An Investment Committee shall be established to assist the Investment 
Officer in managing the investment program and to facilitate direct communication to the 
Governing Body. The Investment Committee shall be composed of the following persons: 
 
1.   
Two members of the City Council appointed by the President of the City Council, for 2-
year terms. These members shall be voting members of the Committee and one member of 
the City Council shall be appointed Chair of the Investment Committee.  
2.   
The Mayor or designee. The Mayor or his/her designee shall be a voting member of the 
Committee. 
3.   
The City Treasurer. The City Treasurer shall be a voting member of the Committee. 
4.   
The Deputy City Treasurer. The Deputy City Treasurer shall be a non-voting member of 
the Committee. 
5.   
The City Attorney or designee. The City Attorney or his/her designee shall be a non-voting 
member of the Committee. 
6.   
Two non-City employees with financial expertise shall be appointed by the Chairman of 
the Committee for 2-year terms. These members shall be non-voting members of the 
Committee. 
7.   
Administrative staff shall be provided by the City Council’s Office. 
 
The Investment Committee shall meet on a biannual basis at a time designated by the Chair of the 
Investment Committee. If needed, the Chair of the Investment Committee can call a special 
meeting. As part of its duties, the Investment Committee shall: 
 
• Undertake a biannual review of the City's investment activities, and recommend changes 
as needed; and 
• On a periodic basis, review, comment upon, and make changes to this Policy. 
 
Investment Advisors:  The City may engage the services of one or more external investment 
advisors/managers to assist in the management of the City’s investment portfolio in a manner 
consistent with the objectives contained herein. Such external managers may be granted discretion 
to purchase and sell investment securities in accordance with this Policy. Such managers must be 
registered under the Investment Advisers Act of 1940 and are subject to all requirements relating 
to investment advisers under Wyoming law. The firm shall have at least a three (3) year verifiable 
track record and a total assets under management (AUM) of at least $500 million. 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
3 
 
 
V. 
Standards of Care 
 
 
All participants in the investment process shall be held to the highest standards of professional and 
ethical conduct. Each participant should be committed to honesty, integrity, and accountability in 
all of his or her decisions and actions relating to the investment process. 
 
Compliance with Laws, Rules, Regulations and Policies:  All participants in the investment 
process must comply with any and all applicable laws, rules and regulations and the terms and 
provisions of this Investment Policy. 
 
Prudence:  The standard of care to be applied by all participants in the investment process shall 
be the “prudent person” standard.   
 
Any person, persons, or third parties investing or managing the City’s financial assets, shall do so 
as a prudent investor would, by considering the purposes, terms, distribution requirements, and 
other circumstances, and exercising reasonable care, skill and caution in all decisions and actions. 
 
 
Conflicts of Interest:  All participants in the investment process have a duty to avoid financial, 
business or other relationships which might cause a conflict with the performance of their duties 
and could reasonably be expected to interfere with their duties or their ability to make unbiased 
and objective recommendations and decisions. Each participant should conduct himself or herself 
so as to avoid even the appearance of such conflicts of interest. 
 
To avoid conflicts of interest, all participants in the investment process shall, at a minimum: 
 
• refrain from personal and business activity that could conflict with proper execution and 
management of the investment program, or that could impair their ability to make impartial 
investment decisions; 
• disclose to the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne any material interest in financial institutions 
with which they conduct business; and 
• refrain from undertaking any personal investment transactions with the same individual 
(e.g., investment advisor) with whom business is conducted on behalf of the City. 
 
Internal Controls:  The City Treasurer shall establish a written system of internal guidelines and 
controls, which shall be reviewed and approved by the investment committee and the City’s 
independent auditor. The controls shall be designed to prevent the loss of public funds arising from 
fraud, employee error, misrepresentation by third parties, unanticipated changes in financial 
markets, or imprudent actions by the Investment Officer and participants in the investment process. 
The guidelines and controls should include, but not be limited to: 
 
• frequent reconciliation of investment and accounting records; 
• written confirmation of transactions; 
• annual independent reviews by an external auditor to assure compliance with this policy;  
• appropriate training for participants in the investment process; and 
• a list of approved financial institutions, depositories, and broker/dealers (see below). 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
4 
 
 
VI. 
Authorized Financial Institutions, Depositories, and Broker/Dealers  
 
The Investment Officer will maintain a list of approved financial institutions, depositories, and 
securities broker/dealers selected by a process that ensures due diligence in the selection process. 
The City will limit all security purchases to financial institutions and broker/dealers on the 
approved lists.  
 
Financial institutions and depositories eligible to transact investment business with the City are 
those listed in W.S. § 9-4-817 and must be federally insured through the Federal Deposit Insurance 
Corporation (FDIC). 
 
• banks incorporated under the laws of Wyoming; 
• national banks; 
• savings and loan associations incorporated under the laws of Wyoming; and 
• federal savings and loan associations and federal savings banks. 
 
All financial institutions who desire to become qualified for investment transactions must supply 
the Investment Officer with the following information, as requested: 
 
• audited financial statements; 
• proof of Financial Industry Regulatory Authority certification; 
• trading resolution; 
• proof of state registration; 
• certification of having read the City's Investment Policy; and 
• depository contracts. 
 
All broker/dealers who desire to become qualified for investment transactions must supply the 
following: 
 
• proof of proper registration with their appropriate regulatory agency;  
• proof of insurance, either through private or regulatory sources, satisfactory to the 
Investment Committee;  
• proof of satisfactory record of past performance; and  
• proof of registration to do business in the State of Wyoming.   
 
An annual review of the financial condition and registration of qualified financial institutions and 
broker/dealers will be conducted by the Investment Officer.  
 
VII. 
Delivery, Payment and Safekeeping 
 
Delivery vs. Payment:  All trades of marketable securities entered into by the City shall be 
conducted on a delivery-versus-payment (DVP) basis to ensure that securities are deposited in 
eligible financial institutions prior to the release of funds. 
 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
5 
 
Safekeeping:  Securities shall be held by an independent third-party custodian institution selected 
by the City. All securities will be evidenced by safekeeping receipts in the City’s name. The City 
must receive a safekeeping receipt on the same day as the deposit is made.   
 
VIII. Authorized Investments and Investment Guidelines 
 
Local Government Investment Pools:  The City will use available local government investment 
pools as the primary means of short-term investment management. These local government 
investment pools as of the approval date of this Investment Policy Statement include:  
 
• Wyoming Cooperative Liquid Assets Securities System (Wyoming CLASS) 
• Wyoming Government Investment Fund (WGIF) 
• WyoSTAR I  
 
The City may also use, at its discretion, other qualified short-term investment vehicles or programs. 
 
Other Investment Types:  The City shall invest only in those securities and investments authorized 
by W.S. § 9-4-831 and any other state law authorizing such investments. 
 
Credit Guidelines: The following credit guidelines shall be followed:   
 
• Bonds must be investment grade rated by at least two of the three Nationally Recognized 
Securities Rating Organizations (“NRSRO”) (Fitch Ratings, Inc., Moody’s Investors 
Service, Inc. and S&P Global Ratings). If the downgrade of a single bond forces the holding 
below the lowest rating allowed for the security, the City’s investment advisor will notify 
the City within a reasonable timeframe and a decision will be made whether to hold or sell 
the bond. 
• No non-investment grade bonds can be held for more than three months after the 
downgrade. No non-investment grade bonds can be bought. 
• Purchases or sales of securities shall be executed through firm(s) offering the best price 
and execution, unless otherwise directed by the City. All fixed income investments will be 
U.S. dollar denominated.  
• All securities in the short term/liquid portfolio that are not backed by the U.S. Government, 
its agencies, or its instrumentalities must conform to Securities and Exchange Commission 
(SEC) requirements for first tier money market investments. 
 
Authorized Collateral:  Collateralization is required for investments in certificates of deposits. To 
reduce market risk, the collateralization level will be 105% of the market value of principal and 
accrued interest, after any applicable FDIC insurance limits. Only U.S. Treasury securities are 
permitted to be used as collateral.  
 
The City Treasurer shall verify monthly that the value of the collateral is sufficient to cover the 
deposits of investments discussed in this Investment Policy Statement. Acceptable instruments for 
collateralization of certificates of deposit shall be the same as set forth in W.S. § 9-4-820 and W.S. 
§ 9-8-821. In addition, collateralization is also required on the following types of investments: 
 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
6 
 
• time deposits; and 
• repurchase and reverse agreements. 
 
IX. 
Investment Parameters 
 
Diversification:  The City shall mitigate credit risk—which is defined as the risk that a security or 
a portfolio will lose some or all of its value due to a real or perceived change in the ability of the 
issuer to repay its debt—by diversifying its investments as follows: 
 
• limiting investments in investment grade securities that have higher credit risks;  
• investing in short-term eligible securities to include institutional certificates of deposit and 
asset-backed commercial paper; 
• investing in securities with varying maturities;   
• limiting individual holdings, other than those backed by the U.S. Government, its agencies, 
or its instrumentalities, certificates of deposits, and authorized pools to no more than 5% 
of the City’s total investment portfolio in any one single obligor; and 
• continuously investing a portion of the portfolio in readily available funds. 
  
Liquidity:  The City shall mitigate market risk—which is defined as the risk that the portfolio value 
will fluctuate due to changes in the general level of interest rates—by providing adequate liquidity 
for short-term cash needs, and by making longer-term investments only with funds that are not 
needed for current cash flow purposes. The investment portfolio shall remain sufficiently liquid to 
meet all operating requirements that may be reasonably anticipated. To further mitigate market 
risk, the following strategies shall be followed: 
  
• the maximum percent of callable securities in the portfolio shall be 20%;  
• liquidity funds will be held in one of the local government investment pools in money 
market instruments maturing one year and shorter;  
• longer term/core funds will be defined as the funds in excess of liquidity requirements; 
• allow the use of money funds; The duration of the portfolio will not exceed +20% of the 
benchmark. 
• no index related funds are allowed. Examples of these prohibited index related securities 
are those whose interest rate is based on an index of commodities or equity securities. 
 
X. 
Performance Standards/Evaluation 
 
The performance of investments will be measured against the performance of a combination of 
benchmarks. Investment performance will be measured using “Total Rate of Return” 
measurement. The manager must provide monthly, quarterly, and annual performance reports 
against the stated benchmark on a net of fees basis.   
 
The assets of the portfolio managed by external advisors shall be benchmarked as follows: 
 
• Longer Term/CORE funds:  65% ICE BofA 1-5 Year Treasury-Agency / 30% ICE BofA 
US Mortgage Backed Securities / 5% Bloomberg US TIPS; 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
7 
 
• Liquidity Funds (excluding WyoSTAR I, Wyoming CLASS and Wyoming Government 
Investment Fund): Citigroup 3-month Treasury Bill Index. 
 
XI. 
Reporting 
 
The City Treasurer is charged with the responsibility of preparing a bi-annual investment report 
and providing it to the Investment Committee and City Governing Body, showing the type of 
investment, institution, rate of interest, maturity date and amount of deposit. This investment report 
will also be posted to the City Treasurer’s public webpage. 
 
XII. 
Statement of Investment Officer or Advisor 
 
Pursuant to W.S. § 9-4-831(h), the following Investment Policy Statement must be signed by any 
person who: 
 
1. 
effects any investment transaction on behalf of a political subdivision; or 
 
2. 
offers any investment to the Governing Body of a political subdivision. 
 
 
“Pursuant to W.S. § 9-4-831(h) and this Investment Policy Statement of the City of Cheyenne, I 
hereby swear and affirm that I have read the Investment Policy Statement of the City of Cheyenne, 
and that I agree to abide by applicable state law with respect to advice I give and the transactions 
I undertake on behalf of the City of Cheyenne.”

Liquidity:  The City shall mitigate market risk—which is defined as the risk that the portfolio value 
will fluctuate due to changes in the general level of interest rates—by providing adequate liquidity 
for short-term cash needs, and by making longer-term investments only with funds that are not 
needed for current cash flow purposes. The investment portfolio shall remain sufficiently liquid to 
meet all operating requirements that may be reasonably anticipated. To further mitigate market 
risk, the following strategies shall be followed: 
  
• a minimum level of six months of budgeted operating expenditures in short term 
investment shall be maintained, unless the budgeted disbursements are held in Wyo-Star I, 
to provide sufficient liquidity for expected disbursements; 
• the maximum percent of callable securities in the portfolio shall be 20%;  
• liquidity funds will be held in the Wyo-Star or one of the local government investment 
pools in money market instruments maturing one year and shorter;  
• longer term/core funds will be defined as the funds in excess of liquidity requirements; 
• allow the use of money funds; The duration of the portfolio will not exceed +20% of the 
benchmark. 
• no index related funds are allowed. Examples of these prohibited index related securities 
are those whose interest rate is based on an index of commodities or equity securities. 
 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
7 
 
X. 
Performance Standards/Evaluation 
 
The performance of investments will be measured against the performance of a combination of 
benchmarks. Investment performance will be measured using “Total Rate of Return” 
measurement. The manager must provide monthly, quarterly, and annual performance reports 
against the stated benchmark on a net of fees basis.   
 
The assets of the portfolio managed by external advisors shall be benchmarked as follows: 
 
• Longer Term/CORE funds:  Bloomberg Barclays U.S. 1-3 Government/Credit Index; 65% 
ICE BofA 1-5 Year Treasury-Agency / 30% ICE BofA US Mortgage Backed Securities / 
5% Bloomberg US TIPS; 
• Liquidity Funds (excluding Wyo-Star): WyoSTAR I, Wyoming CLASS and Wyoming 
Government Investment Fund): Citigroup 3-month Treasury Bill Index. 
 
XI. 
Reporting 
 
The City Treasurer is charged with the responsibility of preparing a bi-annual investment report 
and providing it to the Investment Committee and City Governing Body, showing the type of 
investment, institution, rate of interest, maturity date and amount of deposit. This investment report 
will also be posted to the City Treasurer’s public webpage. 
 
XI.XII. 
Statement of Investment Officer or Advisor 
 
Pursuant to W.S. § 9-4-831(h), the following Investment Policy Statement must be signed by any 
person who: 
 
1. 
effects any investment transaction on behalf of a political subdivision; or 
 
2. 
offers any investment to the Governing Body of a political subdivision. 
 
 
“Pursuant to W.S. § 9-4-831(h) and this Investment Policy Statement of the City of Cheyenne, I 
hereby swear and affirm that I have read the Investment Policy Statement of the City of Cheyenne, 
and that I agree to abide by applicable state law with respect to advice I give and the transactions 
I undertake on behalf of the City of Cheyenne.” 
 
The Client 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The Advisor

ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION GRANTING AN EXCEPTION TO THE '201 
AGREEMENT' FOR THE OWNER OF THE PROPERTY LOCATED

HIGHWAY, BETWEEN ROBERT AVENUE AND DAYSHIA LANE." 
 
WHEREAS, on April 25, 1983, the City of Cheyenne (“City”), the County of Laramie 
("County"), the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities ("BOPU"), and the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District (“District”) entered into an agreement entitled "Memorandum of 
Agreement: Intergovernmental Contract Agreeing to Participate in the Implementation of the 
Findings of the 201 Facilities Plan Final Report for the City of Cheyenne, the South Cheyenne 
Water and Sewer District and Laramie County"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the April 25, 1983, agreement is commonly referred to as the "201 
Agreement"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the respective responsibilities and obligations of 
the signatory governmental entities with respect to the implementation of the "201 Facilities Plan" 
that was created in response to the requirements of Section 201 of the Federal Water Pollution 
Control Act; 
 
 
WHEREAS, one of the principal purposes of the 201 Agreement is to ensure that 
wastewater (sewage) generated in the areas immediately surrounding the corporate limits of the 
City of Cheyenne would preferentially be disposed of through the sanitary sewer systems operated 
by BOPU; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 201 Agreement defines the "service area boundary" sometimes referred 
to as the "sewerable boundary" or "201 Area" as the area that "can be provided with Municipal 
Sewage Treatment Services via gravity flow"; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Applicant is requesting a 201 Exception as owner of a residential property 
that lies within the City of Cheyenne jurisdiction as well as within the 201 Area, specifically Parcel 
Identification No. 1366290001400, which is 10.57-acres in size, is legally described as a portion 
of Section 29, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie County, 
Wyoming, and is more particularly described as follows:

THE NORTH LINE OF THAT PARCEL DESCRIBED IN BOOK 2622, PAGE

SAID SOUTH LINE, 80.02’ TO A THE NORTHWEST COMER OF TRACT 3,

TRACT 3, 427.47’ TO THE WEST R/W LINE OF US HWY 85; TH S.02 DEG 
50'46"E., ALONG SAID WEST LINE, 923.43’ TO THE POB. 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicant seeks to install a small wastewater system [septic system] at a 
cost of approximately $14,500.00 rather than pay an estimated cost of $316,650.00 to extend 
sanitary sewer service approximately 330 linear feet to the Applicant’s property; 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 3.4(d) of the 201 Agreement, Laramie County 
government agreed to work with the City-County Health Unit to "ensure that no new permits for 
small wastewater treatment systems within the service area boundary that are inconsistent with the 
Facility Plan are issued"; 
 
WHEREAS, exceptions for the installation of small wastewater treatment systems may be 
granted pursuant to Section 4.1 of the 201 Agreement, and the Applicant has applied for such an 
exception; 
 
WHEREAS, Section 4.1 provides in relevant part that an exception may be granted if it 
"will alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance with the Facility Plan will 
exacerbate this hardship. Section 4.1 further provides that a "hardship will be determined based 
upon a cost/effectiveness study of sewer service provision to the property seeking the exception;" 
and 
 
 
WHEREAS, upon finding that a demonstrable hardship exists under Section 4.1 of the 201 
Agreement, on March 3, 2025, the City Planning Commission recommended that the Governing 
Body grant the Applicant's 201 Exception to permit the installation of a small wastewater system 
on the subject property. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the City of Cheyenne hereby consents to the granting of an 
exception pursuant to Section 4.1 of the 201 Agreement to permit the installation of a small 
wastewater system, as the exception will alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance 
with the Facility Plan will exacerbate this hardship; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the City of Cheyenne hereby consents to the granting 
of an exception pursuant to Section 4.1 of the 201 Agreement on the condition that the property
