PUDC-25-29 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION(s): 
 
Move to recommend the City Council approve the exception to the 201 Facilities Plan requirement for 
City sewer service, with conditions as listed in the staff report.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
Cheyenne Municipal Code Chapter 1.16 (Annexation and Infrastructure Improvements)  
• 
Cheyenne Municipal Code Chapter 13.12 (Sewer System) 
• 
201 Intergovernmental Agreement to participate in the implementation of the 201 Facilities 
Plan Final Report for the City of Cheyenne, the South Cheyenne Water and Sewer District, and 
Laramie County (1983) (IGA) 
• 
201 Facilities Plan Final Report (1982) 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The request for an exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review. After the 
Commission review, the exception would be approved or denied by the City Council. If the property 
seeking the exception is outside the city's Corporate Limits and inside the 201 Service Area Boundary, 
then County Commissioner approval is also required. 
 
BACKGROUND: 
In 1982 the City, County, and south Cheyenne Water and Sewer District executed an intergovernmental 
agreement to implement the 1982 Wastewater Facilities Master Plan. The plan and the IGA were 
intended to satisfy the Federal Clean Water Act requirements and to protect the safety and quality of 
the region’s groundwater from pollution, specifically from septic system failures.   
 
The IGA requires connection to sewer systems for development of properties lying within the Urban 
Service Area identified in the Plan. The Facilities Plan defines the Urban Service Area as the area where 
“Municipal Sewerage Treatment services can be provided via gravity flow” [Section 1.0, Definitions of 
the IGA]. The IGA further states that the City of Cheyenne is to limit the use of on-site wastewater 
treatment systems within the Urban Service Area; this would include septic systems and package 
wastewater treatment plants. The IGA states that the City and County will not approve any development 
which is inconsistent with the Facility Plan unless granted an exception pursuant to Sections 4.0 to 4.7 of 
the IGA. 
 
The applicant applied for a permit to allow a self-contained septic holding tank. The permit was denied. 
After denial, the applicant was eligible to apply for an exception pursuant to the IGA. The request for an 
exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review. After Commission review, the 
exception would be approved or denied by the City Council and the County Commissioners if the 
property seeking the exception is outside the Corporate Limits of the City and inside the 201 Service 
Area Boundary. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-25-29 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING:

ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve the exception, the Commission must make the 
following findings per Sections 4.0 to 4.7 of the 201 IGA. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
Section 4.0: EXCEPTIONS FOR DEVELOPMENT THAT IS NOT CONSISTENT WITH THE PROPOSED FACILITY 
PLAN: 
 
4.1 Exceptions can be granted for the following reasons: 
(a) The proposed exception conforms with the criteria for exceptions in The City of Cheyenne 
Annexation Policies. 
(b) The exception can be shown not to be detrimental to the public health, safety and welfare [sic]. 
(c) The exception will alleviate a clearly demonstrable hardship and compliance with the Facility Plan 
will exacerbate this hardship. A hardship will be determined based upon a cost/effectiveness study of 
sewer service provision to the property seeking the exception. 
(d) The exception demonstrates substantial compliance with the proposed Facility Plan. 
 
Reason (a): This property is outside the City limits and does not have sanitary sewer readily available. An 
off-site sanitary sewer main extension would be required. The closest BOPU sewer main is in Loving Trail 
approximately a half mile away. This proposal seeks an exception from tying into the sanitary sewer 
system for a future warehouse. A lift station may also be needed. 
 
Reason (b): The proposed facility is on a parcel that is roughly 10.57 acres. Throughout the County, 
septic systems are allowed on 5-acre lots, and approving this exception would be consistent with 
standard County development requirements.  
 
Reason (c): The estimated cost of extending a sewer main is $461,825.00, at this time the applicant was 
unable to provide a cost estimate for the proposed holding tank, but it is highly likely that a septic 
system would be less than 5% of the cost of extending the sewer. 
 
Reason (d): The facility would maintain the principal goal of the IGA – that is to prevent the excessive 
environmental pollution of the land. Typical county development allows septic systems on 5-acre 
parcels, and, if approved, this would establish two septic systems on a 10.57-acre parcel.  
 

PUDC-25-29 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
 
4.2. Exceptions may only be considered if the proposal has been denied due to non-conformance with 
the proposed Facility Plan. 
 
The applicant applied for a permit to allow a small wastewater system on February 10, 2025.  The 
permit was denied on February 11, 2025. After denial, the applicant applied for an exception 
pursuant to the IGA.   
 
4.3. The request for exception must be submitted to the Planning Commission for review. 
 
This proposal is being submitted to the Planning Commission. 
 
4.4. After Planning Commission review, the exception will be approved or disapproved by: 
(a) The City Council if the property seeking exception is within the Corporate Limits; (b) The City 
Council and County Commissioners if the property seeking exception is outside the Corporate Limits 
and inside the Service Area Boundary. 
 
The property seeking an exception is currently outside of the City limits of the City of Cheyenne, and 
the exception will be submitted to the City Council after the Planning Commission review. It has also 
been submitted to the county commissioners.  
 
4.5 (Due to a formatting error in the original Agreement, there is no Section 4.5) 
 
4.6. The replacement of an existing on-site wastewater treatment system on a property that is neither 
contiguous with the Cheyenne Corporate Limits nor within the DISTRICT's boundaries will not require 
an exception under this Agreement. 
 
This Section is not applicable to this proposal. 
 
4.7. The installation of a new on-site wastewater system on a single lot within the Service Area 
Boundary that is not contiguous with the Cheyenne Corporate Limits nor within the DISTRICT's 
boundaries will not require an exception under this Agreement, provided that the lot meets all the 
requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and any other requirements of this Agreement and was 
platted prior to the effective date of this Agreement. 
 
This Section is not applicable to this proposal. 
 
 
RELEVANT PLANCHEYENNE POLICIES  
The following PlanCheyenne polices are related to this project: 
 
Foundation 1—Growing as a Community of Choice 
Policy 1.1.C: Regional Coordination on Land Use and Development Issues. 
 
Foundation 7—Developing in a Fiscally Responsible Way 
Principle 7.1: Our community will coordinate and plan for growth regionally to ensure efficient 
and cost-effective services and utilities. 
Principle 7.2: The Cheyenne Area will provide adequate public facilities and services for current 
and future residents in a fiscally responsible manner. 
 

PUDC-25-29 
 
 
March 3, 2025 
 
The IGA was created to coordinate regionally on environmental health. The proposed small wastewater 
system provides a service commonly available to parcels that do not have readily available sewers. This 
proposal is to place a warehouse on the property, allowing the property to be usable. The extension of 
the sewer is cost prohibitive for this style of development. 
The exception request complies with the goals of PlanCheyenne.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
The staff’s analysis is that the application meets the requirements to qualify for a 201 Exception. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend the Governing Body approve the exception as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend the Governing Body approve the exception subject to Staff’s recommended 
conditions (Staff Recommendation). 
3. Recommend denial of  the exception based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission must 
conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings 
for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the item until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to Staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Planning Commission acts 
contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review 
criteria) for a recommendation to approve or deny.   
 
STAFF’S RECOMMENDATION(s): 
Staff recommends the Planning Commission recommend approval of the exception to the 201 Facilities 
Plan requirement for City sewer service to be extended to this property and permit installation of a 
septic holding system, subject to the following condition: 
1. At such time that a Board of Public Utilities’ sanitary sewer line is extended to the property, the 
property owner shall connect to available City utilities meeting all applicable requirements.  
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Master Application  
2. Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Denial Letter 
3. Cost estimated for sewer

support this designation:

2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The plat application uses sound planning and urban design principles by aligning with existing 
development patterns and allowing for future developments. It ensures compatibility with 
current policies and plans for infrastructure connectivity and accessibility, and has minimal 
impacts on public utilities and facilities, and applies urban design principles to enhance 
functionality, aesthetics, and livability. It is not detrimental to the schools which are nearby. 
This application includes opportunities for connections to the east, as the east end of the plat is 
a road. The application proposes extending utility infrastructure to the east, opening 
opportunities for development. The open space network proposed reflects sound urban design 
principles.  
 
There are some slope constraints to integrating with nearby development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.

For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. Land 
reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are exempt 
from fees. Areas that have previously paid park fees can use the previous payment as a credit 
toward fees due at the time of the current plat. There are 7.5 acres of land for which plat fees

$5,250 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition and infrastructure fee is payable at the 
time the final plat is signed.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at issuing a building permit for 
each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 

PUDC-25-3  
 
 
March 3, 2025 
This plat is in an area with previous development and slope constraints. The ability to provide 
appropriately sized blocks on the north and south edges are limited. The blocks within this 
plat are larger than the maximum allowed, but they are intersected by pedestrian connections 
and open spaces. The oversize blocks on this plat qualify for the exception under the Natural 
Features & Open Space System. 
 
Open Space (4.4): 
The proposed Final Plat consists of 112 dwelling units. The intent is to use the lots listed as 
Common Open Space Lots on this plat combined with previous platted lots in Harmony Valley 
1st Filing listed as open space. The HOA of Harmony Valley maintains the open space. This plat 
will be joining the HOA as well so maintenance is handled. Owners will also have the luxury of 
using the park space previously platted Lot 1, Block 7 of Harmony Valley 1st Filing. This lot has a 
football field, basketball court, 2 pickleball courts, as well as playground equipment and 
restrooms. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat seems well-equipped to deliver all necessary enhancements and utilities. However, the 
proposed BOPU facilities within the plat boundary will require easements wherever the right-of-
way for the mains is insufficient. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any residential building on any of 
the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable when a building 
permit is issued for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
 
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No phasing is proposed with this project. There is a remainder lot on the east side of this project 
– but the preliminary plat does not include a formal development pattern for this area. A 
separate preliminary plat will be required to develop the land to the east. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
Drainage: 
A drainage study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the study provided. 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the study provided. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 

PUDC-25-3  
 
 
March 3, 2025 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed application occurs in concert with a proposal to zone the land NR-3 Neighborhood 
Residential – High Density. The NR-3 zone is intended for a mix of high-density residential uses 
in a compact development pattern (with smaller, walkable blocks). The NR-3 zone allows for 
Cottage Lot development – a scenario in which multiple single-unit lots have access to a 
common open area.  All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
This plat does propose unbuildable lots for common open space and drainage. The applicant has indicated 
maintenance of these properties would be via the Harmony Valley HOA, as stated in the Open Space 
narrative. The applicant stated that the ownership of these lots would be by undivided interest.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. Development 
currently exist to the north, west and south of the location. This project extends infrastructure 
to the east.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
The plat clearly indicates the remaining lots (Lot 1 and 2, and block 10 will be subdivided in the 
future).  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

PUDC-25-3  
 
 
March 3, 2025 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
During the review process for the Preliminary Plat application, the staff has identified minor 
corrections that should be addressed. These adjustments are recommended as conditions for 
approval and must be resolved before the final plat can be recorded. 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
Since this is a combined Preliminary and Final Plat application, the review criterion for the Final 
Plat is addressed by the corresponding criterion used in the Preliminary Plat review (as outlined 
in subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.    
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED: 
 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the 
review criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways: 
- 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on February 15, 2025 
- 
Posted Notice Evidence of posted notice was not provided at the time of the staff report. (not 
required until February 24, 2025) 
- 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on February 11, 2025 
 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (February 24, 2025), Staff has received no inquiries or 
comments from the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 30 views and 
no comments. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
- 
Technical map issues (addressed via revised submittal) 
- 
Alley naming (addressed via revised submittal) 

PUDC-25-3  
 
 
March 3, 2025 
- 
Functionality of the alleys (addressed via revised submittal)  
- 
Future greenway construction (to be addressed with future application) 
- 
Block sizes (exception proposed) 
- 
Water and Sewer Layouts and (partly addressed by revised submittal) 
 
CONCLUSION: 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant with conditions.  
 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body, with the following exception and condition: 
1. The following exception is to be granted:  
a. Exception to UDC 4.3.3.c, regarding minimum block size and connectivity 
2. Prior to approval of the final plat the following shall be required: 
a. Provide appropriate easements for the proposed BOPU facilities on the face of the plat, 
including the applicable note. 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat (including a utility plan and a grading plan) 
5. Final Plat

An expedited review is when both a preliminary
and final plat are submitted to the Planning
Commission together. To be eligible for expedited
review the preliminary plat shall contain no
significant planning, design, and engineering
issues. Upon determination by staff, the applicant
may submit a preliminary and final plat to the
Planning Commission.*
I acknowledge the that Expedited status is not
guaranteed.*
Written Statement*
We are submitting an expedited plat for 112 residential lots for the Harmony
Valley 5 Filing, which contains 44.14 acres.   The plat is designed using NR-3
Zoning and alley load products.  Open space is being shown and will be placed
into the HOA for Harmony Valley.  The HOA will be providing the maintenance of
the Open space, which contains 3.55 Acres and is contained with 11 lots.
2 remainder lots will be platted to be subdivided in the future and are contained
within Block 10.

AND WEST OF SOUTH GREELEY HIGHWAY." 
 
 
WHEREAS, Thunder Basin Land Co, LLC, is the owner of real property located in 
Laramie County, Wyoming, more specifically described as: Tracts 4 through 8, Cheyenne Power 
Park; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Thunder Basin ("the Applicant") submitted an application to the City of 
Cheyenne and the Board of Public Utilities ("BOPU") for water and sanitary sewer services outside 
of the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne for the above-referenced properties; 
 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 3577 (adopted Sept. 26, 1994), BOPU is instructed 
to present to the Governing Body for its review any application for the provision of water and/or 
sanitary sewer services to any individual or entity outside the corporate limits of the City of 
Cheyenne; 
 
 
WHEREAS, BOPU has reviewed the application submitted by the Applicant, and BOPU 
and the Applicant will approve and execute applicable Outside User Agreement(s) upon approval 
by the Governing Body pursuant to Resolution No. 3577; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the application for sanitary sewer services 
and water services submitted by the Applicant and has determined that approval is in the best 
interest of the City of Cheyenne, provided that the proposed development will comply with City 
Standards, or applicable City relief processes.  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the application submitted by the Applicant to BOPU for water 
and sanitary sewer services outside of the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne for the property 
more specifically described above is hereby approved subject to the following conditions: 1) The 
proposed development shall comply with City Standards, or applicable City relief processes; and, 
2) The property owner shall initiate annexation pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-403 no later than 
September 1, 2026. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that BOPU and the Applicant are authorized to approve 
and execute Outside User Agreement(s), subject to the conditions imposed by the Governing Body.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-25-33: Cheyenne Power Park - Outside User Agreement
REQUEST:
An outside user agreement for the provision of sewer and water to properties
outside City limits.
LOCATION:
South of High Plains Road and west of South Greeley Highway.
APPLICANT:
Thunder Basin Land CO LLC
AGENT:
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning
OWNERS:
Thunder Basin Land CO LLC
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-25-33 
 
 
March 10, 2025 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
x 
Resolution 3577 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting connection to BOPU sanitary sewer services and water services for property 
outside City limits. It is City Staff’s understanding that the property is intending to develop using a funding 
source that is unavailable if the property is within City limits. Subsequent to securing funding, the owner 
would then annex into the City. This property is currently mostly vacant but is proposed to have multiple 
warehouses. There is currently no existing sewer in the area or other outside user agreements. City water is 
currently adjacent to the property along the north side.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned a PUD Planned Unit Development in the County.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
The request for an outside user agreement has no formal review criteria; however, the following 
elements should be considered when reviewing outside user agreements: 
- 
BOPU capacity 
- 
Needed infrastructure improvements 
- 
Population served 
- 
Considerations on the effect on future annexation(s) 
- 
Fiscal cost/benefit to the City and the BOPU 
- 
Conformance with the Comprehensive Plan 
 
The requested outside user agreement has minimal impact on BOPU’s system capacity. The proposed 
use of the properties is for industrial warehousing. Sewer will need to be developed in the area and 
connected into South Cheyenne Water and Sewer district at the intersection of High Plains Road and 
South Greeley. Water is available within High Plains Road, but would need to be extended into the 
development.  
 
Property tax would increase, but the City would not have any income from the property tax in the area 
at this time. The BOPU may charge a higher rate for outside users. The sewer infrastructure will need to

PUDC-25-33 
 
 
March 10, 2025 
be extended to this area as it does not exist already, and Developer will be responsible for extending the 
infrastructure. City police, fire, and sanitation services are within this area as High Plains Road and the 
large 943 acre parcel to the northwest are both within City Limits. Staff’s understanding is that this 
property will be annexed into the City once funds are secured. The funds looking to be secured are only 
available if the development takes place outside City limits. Staff recommends a condition that the 
applicant annex pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-403 no later than September 1, 2026.  
 
The Comprehensive Plan designates this area as Mixed-use Residential – and the proposed use in this 
location is consistent with that anticipated development pattern. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is discussed above.    
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
x 
Planning and Engineering  
o The proposed development will comply with City Standards, or applicable City relief 
processes. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application is acceptable based on the considerations specific to this request 
and therefore recommends approval of the outside user agreement with the conditions that the 
proposed development shall comply with City Standards, or applicable City relief processes and that the 
property submit for annexation no later than September 1, 2026.    
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the proposed outside user agreement subject to the following conditions:  
1. The proposed development shall comply with City Standards, or applicable City relief processes.  
2. The property owner initiates annexation pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-403 no later than 
September 1, 2026.

2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Application 
4. Applicant-submitted proposed site plan

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-03-04_TotalExtraction_90942636.pdf
Agreement By and Between Cheyenne Civic Center and I.A.T.S.E. Local 229 
Page 1 of 4 
 
Agreement By and Between 
Cheyenne Civic Center and I.A.T.S.E. Local 229 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into by the Cheyenne Civic Center (hereinafter referred to as 
the “Producer”) and Local 229 of the International Alliance of Theatrical Stage Employees, Moving Picture 
Technicians, Artists and Allied Crafts of the United States, its Territories and Canada (hereinafter referred to 
as the “Local 229”). 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties hereto, intending to be legally bound, hereby agree as follows: 
 
1. 
RECOGNITION 
A. The Producer acknowledges that Local 229 shall exclusively represent and refer all requested 
stagehand, rigging and operator employees during the term of this agreement. The covered work 
includes: all rigging (operation of all: boom lifts, lull lifts, AWPs / MEWPs), truss installation, 
lighting, carpentry, properties, audio, video, truck loaders, forklift op, hair/make-up, and 
wardrobe labor as required. Operation of production eq: lighting and audio boards, cameras, 
spotlights, flyrail, etc. as required. Any venue equipment that is required to be used by the show 
that needs a certified operator, the venue will supply the operator if available. 
B. The Producer acknowledges that upon any request for more stagehand labor, as defined in section 
1.A of this agreement, that Local 229 shall exclusively represent the additional labor during the 
term of this agreement. 
C. The Producer will place all calls for labor under this agreement with Local 229 and will not 
contact the employees directly. Local 229 will make every effort to refer all workers requested 
by the Producer and will work closely with the Producer’s representative to integrate Producer’s 
current call list with Local 229’s call list and employee referral procedure. If Local 229 requests 
assistance from the Producer to fill a specific call, then the Producer may contact employees for 
that specific call. 
D. This agreement shall be for work at the Cheyenne Civic Center Performing Arts Theater and be 
for productions that require 22 or more stagehands by the incoming production. At the discretion 
of the Producer, Local 229 can be used for smaller calls (less than 22 stagehands) and at any other 
venue the producers may choose and all provisions of 1.A, 1.B and 1.C will be followed along 
with the remainder of this document. 
E. For this agreement the terms: employee, worker, or referent will be used synonymously. 
 
2. 
ENGAGEMENTS 
A. Local 229 agrees to refer competent employees to perform work as required by the Producer 
under the provisions of this Agreement. 
B. The Producer shall provide a minimum of twelve (12) hours notice of a change in time or 
cancellation of a call. Local 229, upon receipt of such notice, shall make a reasonable effort to 
notify all employees of the change or cancellation. If any employee does not receive notice and 
reports for work at the previously assigned time, the Producer shall pay six (6) hours wages to 
any employee who reports for work as originally scheduled. Any mileage will also be paid as 
required by this agreement. All cancellations within twelve (12) hours of the first scheduled call 
will result in the Producer being presented an invoice for six (6) hours wages for each employee 
scheduled and any travel allowance if applicable will be one-half (1/2) of the amounts described 
11:28 am, Feb 19, 2025

Agreement By and Between Cheyenne Civic Center and I.A.T.S.E. Local 229 
Page 2 of 4 
 
in the IATSE 229 and Cheyenne Civic Center Rate Sheet, attached hereto as Exhibit A and 
incorporated by this reference. 
C. Travel Allowance: Travel Allowance will only be paid if the referred worker completes all calls 
that have been accepted. Travel Allowance is only one (1) per qualified employee in any 24-hour 
workday. This is not a wage, but an allowance and not subject to withholding taxes and 
assessment calculations. Travel per-diem applies for any employee traveling forty-five (45) miles 
or more one way to the venue. If the referent only accepts or only shows up for an In or an Out 
call, the amount of the travel allowance if applicable will be one-half (1/2) of the amounts 
described in the attached  Rate Sheet (Exhibit A). 
D. Any employee who travels greater than one hundred (100) miles one way to the venue and has a 
subsequent call at the same venue or within fourteen (14) hours of the conclusion of the most 
recent workday, must be offered a single occupancy hotel room for safety concerns. Any 
employees traveling greater than one hundred twenty-five (125) miles one way to a venue or must 
be offered a single occupancy hotel room for safety concerns, regardless of any subsequent work 
or not. Any and all of this lodging will be arranged and pre-paid for by the Producer. 
E. The Producer shall be responsible for all wages, associated payroll fees and other obligations. 
 
3. 
WAGES AND CONDITIONS 
A. Please refer to the attached Rate Sheet (Exhibit A). 
B. All work calls shall be a minimum of six (6) hours. Any break given before the end of the fourth 
(4th) hour shall be a paid break. A continuation call may be scheduled for one (1) hour after the 
beginning of an unpaid break and shall be for a minimum of two (2) hours. "Load-In", "Load-
Out" and "Show" are each separate work calls. 
C. All time worked after eight (8) hours in one workday shall be paid at a rate of one and one-half 
times (1½ x) the base rate. All time worked between the hours of 12:00AM and 8:00AM shall be 
paid at a rate of one and one-half times (1½ x) the base rate. A new workday shall begin eight (8) 
hours or more after the completion of the most recent work period. No worker shall be replaced 
for the purpose of avoiding payment of overtime. Local 229 shall not refuse to relieve any 
personnel who may not be able to work safely due to fatigue. 
D. The following holidays will be paid at a rate of two times (2x) the base rate: New Years Day, 
Martin Luther King Jr. Day, Memorial Day, Independence Day, Labor Day, Veterans Day, 
Thanksgiving Day, Christmas Day. All time worked after eight (8) hours or between the hours of 
12:00AM and 7:00AM on any of these holidays will be paid at a rate of three times (3x) the base 
rate. 
E. All work shall be scheduled such that every worker receives a one (1) hour meal break at or before 
the end of any sixth (6th) consecutive hour of work. Paid breaks of one-half (½) hour may 
substitute for a one (1) hour unpaid break if the Producer provides a substantial meal to the 
employee. Any time worked in violation of this provision shall be paid at a meal-penalty rate of 
two times (2x) the current rate until such meal break is given. 
F. Hours shall be tracked in half-hour (½ hour) increments beginning on an exact hour or half-hour. 
Any portion of a half-hour worked will constitute a half-hour worked. 
G. A show call, for a performance or rehearsal, shall begin one-half (½) hour before the announced 
curtain time. A show employee may be scheduled for a continuity call of either one-half (½) hour 
or one (1) hour before the show call to perform work associated with the performance or rehearsal. 
The employee shall be paid for the continuity call in addition to the show call and at one and one-
half times (1½ x) the employee’s prevailing rate. 

Agreement By and Between Cheyenne Civic Center and I.A.T.S.E. Local 229 
Page 3 of 4 
 
H. Wardrobe workers on the show call may work for up to ten (10) minutes after the end of the show 
to finish up. Any work beyond this ten (10) minute period places the worker into the separately 
defined “Load-Out” call. 
I. For trucks of length greater than twenty-four (24) feet that contain production, staging, and/or 
other event related items, four (4) truck loaders are required for the labor call. Truck loaders only 
load and unload trucks. 
 
4. 
PAYMENT 
A. The Producer will use a payroll agent. Payment in full to the payroll agent is due as soon as 
approved by the City on its regular payment schedule. Local 229 will do everything possible to 
expedite the presentation of the invoice and paperwork to the payroll agent. 
B. As these are wages for workers, any invoice must be paid promptly: by mail, by expedited service, 
by courier, in person, or electronically. If the payment for the final presented invoice is not 
received by the payroll agent in full within fourteen (14) business days, there will be a ten percent 
(10%) penalty assessed in the form of a subsequently presented final invoice, which is due upon 
receipt, rendering the originally presented final invoice null. This late penalty of ten percent 
(10%) is applicable for every seven (7) business days that the invoice remains in arrears. This ten 
percent (10%) penalty is an increase in the wages due to each worker expecting payment. The 
subsequent invoices will also include any additional administrative charges required by the 
payroll agent. 
C. The payroll agent chosen shall deduct or have deducted from each employee’s paycheck any 
fees owed by the employee to Local 229. Fees owed are a five percent (5%) assessment 
withholding from the employee’s gross wage. The payroll agent shall deliver or have delivered 
to Local 229, P.O. Box 677, Fort Collins, CO 80522 said fees, along with a report of the gross 
income and amount collected from each employee. These fees and reports are able to be sent 
electronically to Local 229. Fees shall be delivered on the same schedule as paychecks. 
 
5. 
BENEFITS 
A contribution to the IATSE NBF (National Benefit Fund) is required for each employee covered by 
this agreement. See Rate Sheet (Exhibit A) for applicable percentage. Full contribution is submitted 
to the Annuity. 
 
6. 
TERM OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement shall remain in effect beginning upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto 
until and including the 30th day of June 2026. With negotiations in the Spring/Summer of 2026.
